# Skills · C · part 178

881 entries, cor-design to CoreDNS Custom Configuration. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **cor-design**: Exploratory UI design phase of the COR methodology. Captures visual goals and constraints, generates three comparable lightweight web variants, scores them, and produces a decision-ready direction before implementation planning. Use when the user wants to explore visual directions, compare UI options, redesign a screen or app shell, or needs a design direction settled before cor-plan. Claude Code users: invoke as /cor:cor-design. ([louisbrillet/cor-skills](https://github.com/louisbrillet/cor-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/cor/skills/cor-design) · ★4)
- **cor-docs**: Updates a project's Markdown documentation from a git diff between two branches plus the existing codebase, following the Diataxis framework (tutorials, how-to guides, reference, explanation). Edits only the quadrants the change actually affects, asks the user when intent is missing, and keeps output compatible with MkDocs and Zensical. Initializes the docs directory (default docs/) when absent. Use when the user asks to document a branch, update docs from a diff or PR, sync documentation with code changes, set up Diataxis docs, or mentions Diataxis, MkDocs, or Zensical. Claude Code users: in… ([louisbrillet/cor-skills](https://github.com/louisbrillet/cor-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/cor/skills/cor-docs) · ★4)
- **cor-plan**: Plan phase of the COR methodology. Takes the understanding built in the Think phase and turns it into a structured, ordered task list saved to .cor/plan.md (or agent memory / session tasks). No coding happens here — only decomposition and sequencing. Claude Code users: invoke as /cor:cor-plan. ([louisbrillet/cor-skills](https://github.com/louisbrillet/cor-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/cor/skills/cor-plan) · ★4)
- **cor-refacto**: Think+Plan skill for behavior-preserving refactoring. Use when the user wants to improve readability, simplicity, maintainability, duplication, or coupling without functional changes. Produces a concrete refactor plan and a strict handoff for cor-code; blocks theoretical refactors that do not reduce net complexity. Claude Code users: invoke as /cor:cor-refacto. ([louisbrillet/cor-skills](https://github.com/louisbrillet/cor-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/cor/skills/cor-refacto) · ★4)
- **cor-research**: Researches current libraries, version-specific APIs, architecture practices, and implementation options after COR Think and before COR Plan. Use when implementation depends on maintained third-party packages, recent platform capabilities, external standards, security guidance, or an evidence-based architecture choice. ([louisbrillet/cor-skills](https://github.com/louisbrillet/cor-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/cor/skills/cor-research) · ★4)
- **cor-review**: Measured code review that ends in a behavior-preserving refactor plan. Combines cor-refacto's simplification gates with deterministic metrics from Uncle Bob's crapper, dryer and mutator CLIs (CRAP per function, structural duplication, mutation testing). Use when the user asks to review or audit a codebase for refactoring, asks what is worth refactoring, mentions complexity, coverage, duplication, mutation score, CRAP, dryer, crapper, or mutator, or wants a refactor plan backed by numbers. Writes no code; hands off to cor-code. Claude Code users: invoke as /cor:cor-review. ([louisbrillet/cor-skills](https://github.com/louisbrillet/cor-skills/tree/HEAD/plugins/cor/skills/cor-review) · ★4)
- **cor-setup**: Initialize a project for the COR (CodingOnRails) methodology. Detects the tech stack, proposes check commands, and writes a .cor/config.md file.
